We in the Group of the European People's Party wanted the police to exercise more control over the latter, especially if people are taking the weekly rest in the rest places, and we would like to see control increased. We have also asked the Committee on Legal Affairs and Citizens' Rights why little more has been done in this area. It would be nice if the Minister of Transport would help to push a little so that there are some answers there and we will have to deal with it. But of course this 25-hour time limit must also be followed by increased parking charges if it is to have the desired effect. And there we can discuss whether what is planned for now is quite enough. But I think it makes sense at least to try, and it is therefore very important now to prevent the time limit here and the increased charges not only from moving the problems to other parts of the road network • that we also get the fines lifted. We must increase control and extend the ban to cover the rest of the road network, including the municipal road network. So what is in the proposal here is Enhedslisten completely on board. And the increase in the fine for parking on the motorway network from 1,000 to 3,000 DKK. We're really happy about that, too. So all in all, this is a really good proposal, a good contribution to the fight against social dumping on the roads. At the same time, it is also proposed that the Danish works council should take control of parking on the state road network, also at rest places for the Road Directorate. And there we also think that it is assumed that the funds will follow fully from the Road Directorate. I hope then that the Minister can confirm in a moment that they will do so, because it is of course important that we can fully fund this work so that it will work very well and as intended. ParlaMint-DK_20181011104530	Thank you, sir. Andreas Steenberg. And we have actually reached the Minister for Transport, Building and Housing. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20181011104539	Thank you. Before I begin my actual speech here, I should like to reply to Mr. Andreas Steenberg on the objection that the legislation says that drivers should be able to take a 45-hour regular rest, and where should they keep it if they are not to keep it in rest places? I have to do sir. Andreas Steenberg is aware that the same legislation says that it is illegal to keep it 45-hour rest in rest places, and that it is therefore a quite unspeakable objection that they should not do so when we introduce the parking restriction. They are not allowed to do so in advance under the legislation which Mr. Andreas Steenberg is referring to. In connection with the negotiations on the Finance Act 2018 it was agreed between the government and the Danish People's Party that temporary parking should be introduced at the state resting places for trucks. As a follow-up to the Finance Act, the government and the Danish People's Party decided in February this year that trucks may park 25 hours maximum in state rest areas. At the same time, it was decided that sanctions should be lifted in order to ensure that the time limit decided is respected. With the decision to introduce the time limit, there is a dispute that the state resting areas are used by truck drivers to stay longer and thus may become a sort of campsite. The 25-hour time limit itself was effective on 1 July 2018. The time limit has therefore already been introduced. Now, with this bill, the government wants to make sure that it is also respected. With the bill, the government wants to raise the general parking charge for vehicles whose permissible maximum weight exceeds 3,500 kg. Today, regardless of the size of the vehicle, the parking charge is DKK 510. This is not the case, however, if you park outside the entrance and exit to and from buildings or are improperly parked in places reserved for disabled people. Here is the penalty DKK 1,020. Now we propose that the general tax be increased from the current DKK 510. DKK 2,040. for vehicles with a maximum permissible weight of more than 3,500 kg, i.e. a quadruple of the tax. The quadruple must reflect that it is the government's assessment that the current level of DKK 510. is too low, as an infringement is, as a general rule, committed by business actors. In other words, the tax increase must be so high that it is taken seriously and has the necessary preventive effect. In addition, we propose that the increase should apply to the entire road network and not only in order to keep parked for longer than 25 hours in the rest areas of the state. This is because we should like to avoid a situation in which lorries are seeking to park on the rest of the road network in order to avoid the higher parking charge in the rest areas. With the proposal, we ensure that it will not be better economically to keep in conflict with the rules on municipal roads. At the same time as the general increase in the parking charge for vehicles with a maximum authorised weight of more than 3,500 kg, it is also proposed that the fine for the same vehicles should generally be increased from DKK 1,000. for 3,000 kroner. In the event of illegal parking on access ramps and exit ramps on the motorway network, an increase will also apply to access ramps and exit ramps on motorway routes. Parking on access ramps and exit ramps may constitute a traffic-dangerous situation, which should also be reflected in the size of the penalty in relation to a parking charge by, for example, keeping too long parked in rest areas. That is why the fine is set at DKK 3 000, so it is higher than in the event of an infringement of a stop or parking ban, where the fine is DKK 1,000. In the context of the 2018 Finance Act, money has been allocated up to 2021 for the administration and control of compliance, including the new 25-hour limitation. The task of monitoring is currently carried out by the Road Directorate. However, it is assessed that the task is best carried out in the long term by the Danish Transport Authority, as the Authority already administers rules in the heavy goods vehicle sector, including the Authority also carries out certain control tasks in relation to the truck industry. That is why we are also proposing that the parking check be taken over in whole or in part by the Danish Transport Authority as far as the roads are concerned. It is the intention, provided that the proposal is adopted by the Danish Parliament, that from 1 January 2019 Færdselsstyrelsen will take over the control task from the Vejdirektoratet. In conclusion, I look forward to the further examination of the proposal and, of course, I am ready to answer any questions that the members of the committee might have on the proposal. Thank you.

ParlaMint-DK_20181011110849	Thank you, sir. Andreas Steenberg. Then it's the Minister of Transport, Building and Housing. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20181011110901	Thank you for that, and thank you to the rapporteurs for the positive reception of the draft law. The aim of the bill is to ensure a more flexible use of the business port and quay sections in Copenhagen's External North Harbour. Since the law in force lays down very detailed rules for the use of land, it can therefore be amended only with the consent of the Danish Parliament. In order to meet the high demand on the market for cruise ships, the bill must include: provide the possibility that the full quay section of Oceankaj of a total of 1,800 m can be used for cruise traffic, etc. Today it is only possible to use 1,100 m for cruise activities out of the total quay section. In addition, the draft law should allow a fourth cruise terminal to be established north of the three existing terminals at Oceankaj. In addition, in order to allow more flexible use of the land for filling up in the Outer North Harbour, the draft law should make it possible for the filling up to be made up of two areas in the future, against today's three areas: an area for business port and an area for maritime and recreational facilities. At the same time, this means a slight change in the division, so that the total area is allocated to the industrial port etc. the area for maritime and recreational facilities is increased, and the use of this area is extended for the benefit of the city's citizens. The change in the division makes it possible to use the land released • approx. 71⁄2 ha towards the existing cruise terminals for urban development etc. This urban development will, of course, take place according to the usual planning processes in the City of Copenhagen with consultations etc. Before I finish, I have to answer questions from the many rapporteurs who spoke about the importance of getting land flow to the cruise ships. I fully agree that this is an important problem to resolve. When they produce power out there with their engines, their diesel engines, they send particles beyond the population of Copenhagen, and therefore it would be better if they got power from land so that they could avoid sending out those particles. The problem here is not that there are too few land power plants. At least it would not solve the problem that we set up a lot of land power plants. The problem is that the ships are not equipped to receive power from land. Nine out of ten ships cannot receive landflows, which means that if we made the requirement that they should be able to receive landflows, nine out of ten ships would not go to Copenhagen, but for example. instead at Malmø. And we don't get fewer particles out of that; we just get the particles discharged into Malmø, and then they fly with the wind to Copenhagen and do the same damage. But we are getting less tourism and less revenue from cruise tourism in Copenhagen. That is why the solution is, after all, the one that the government has put forward in its climate initiative, and in which everyone interested in the proposal could have been aware of, namely that we are starting work on the other countries and cities of the Baltic Sea region, where we will jointly demand that cruise ships calling at our cities should be able to receive land flow. If we do so in this way and get such an agreement, it will have an effect, whereas the second thing that we in Copenhagen alone have to demand landflows is a symbolic effect, namely that the ships will then instead dock in other ports in the Baltic Sea and that we will still have the particles in Copenhagen.

We should not just stand in a pillow because we want to appear as if we are wearing a halo, and then we hunt cruise ships to dock in Malmø or elsewhere, instead of them docking in Copenhagen, and at the same time get exactly the same number of particles as we do today, where they dock in Copenhagen.

ParlaMint-DK_20181011111718	We are opening the debate with the Social Democrat rapporteur, Mr. Kaare Dybvad. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20181011111734	Thank you for that, President. Socialist housing policy is basically based on two objectives. Firstly, we must ensure that as many people as possible have up-to-date and affordable accommodation available, and secondly, we must ensure that we have some residential neighbourhoods that are mixed across social groups and across housing forms. We in the Social Democratic Party believe that a strong welfare society can only be maintained if children from the working class and middle class are classed with children from resourceless families. The effect of having classmates from well-functioning families is crucial for the children who have the lowest starting point. The welfare in our optics can not only be the help you get at the municipality. It is first and foremost the community that is between people in everyday life. That is why social democracy is also included in the agreement that lies behind the legislative proposal we are debating today. It will provide more condominiums in Tingbjerg, it will provide more general housing on Strandvejen. It is a step towards more mixed neighbourhoods in our cities. The agreement is an extension of a long social democratic planning tradition, which extends back to Stauning's law on reorganisation in 1939. It was used at the time for example. to clear Borgergade and Adelgade in central Copenhagen. The agreement is also an extension of what was called the world's best restoration law from 1959, which was used to sank the black square on Nørrebro. It was 15 minutes that got that name because in the office of the social services, a black pin was placed for every child case that was in the area. And that area was totally black. The agreement is also an extension of the urban renewal initiated by the Social Democratic Party in the 80s and 90s, which changed Vesterbro in Copenhagen and changed many other neighbourhoods around the country. Out with the old stove and the damp chambers, came central heating and modern insulation. It has been social democratic policy for most of a century that it is not acceptable for us to place them in our most difficult society in the same areas. In all cases it has been the case that the bourgeois people have thought it was too expensive and that the extreme left has been against it. But neither the bourgeois nor the extreme left will today claim that we should have left it alone. Most people can see what is reasonable in the changes, most people do not want to return to the time before the urban renewal, i.e. before the changes at Nørrebro and in the inner city of Copenhagen. The law we are discussing today is therefore also based on the plan for the Gellerup Park, which the social democratic city government led in Aarhus. It was about building new owners' homes in the area, moving municipal jobs there, setting up a local police station and getting private investors into the area. But it was also about making demolitions where the district closed itself. In the same way as in Gellerup, we now ask 15 of the other vulnerable residential areas to make a fundamental change. In many of the areas, for example: Vollsmose, Tingbjerg, Bispeparken and Taastrupgaard, work is already under way. For the rest, this will be the start of the work. Part of the criticism in the hearing answers is about where people should move. This is an objection that is real enough. In the Social Democratic Party we have therefore made provision for the possibility of using the income from the sale of outside areas and the sale of housing to build new general housing in areas where it would otherwise be too expensive. It is crucial for the Social Democrats that much more housing and much more cheap housing are being built in our large cities, and the bill here can be a step on the way to securing it. Another objection that has been raised is the criticism of reducing the proportion of general family homes to a maximum of 40%. is a decline in population or where there is a very large debt in the residential areas where it will be difficult to get down to 40%. general family residences. We simply cannot use all the tools that have been made available. In Korsør, for example, there is a residential area where you cannot get many private investors in, because there is a population decline and has been for many years. That is why we have made a derogation for those areas where the full palette of tools cannot be used, as can be done, for example. Aarhus or Copenhagen. On the other hand, I must also say that there are some of the criticisms that have been made which I think have been mistaken. The idea that this would be an agreement that would result in much fewer general housing and would close many general housing, I believe, is mistaken. Experience has been gained whether you are talking about social democratic municipalities such as Aarhus or bourgeois municipalities like Høje-Taastrup • that you build the same number of general housing as you tear down. In Høje-Taastrup 188 general dwellings were demolished and a similar number were built. And so do you in Aarhus. So none of our experience so far suggests that this should mean the removal of many ordinary dwellings. On the contrary, experience is that the same number or even more general housing is maintained. So I would say that that part of the criticism seems to me to be exaggerated and perhaps more a sign of the upcoming election campaign than so much of what this bill says. With those words, I must inform you that the Social Democratic Party supports the bill.

The legislative proposal here, as several rapporteurs have mentioned, is the implementation of the agreement we reached back in May with a view to combating parallel societies in Denmark. There must be law and order everywhere in Denmark. It must be the norm for children to learn Danish in Denmark. There should be no areas in Denmark where the norm is to be on public support. For too many years, the challenges facing some heavily burdened residential areas have not been properly addressed. Let's do it now. With the agreement we have made to combat parallel societies, we ensure that the last ghetto in Denmark is completed by 2030. We are opening up the vulnerable residential areas so that the areas become more coherent with the surrounding society and a more mixed housing mix is created, so that no children grow up in areas where it is not the norm to speak Danish or where it is not the norm to be part of the labour market. I have a sincere desire for a future where it is not a them and us, but only a us here in Denmark. That must be the ambition. This requires that you accept that you have to live by Danish values and speak Danish. We must have a coherent society where we do not have enclosed enclaves where there is parallel society to the Danish. I am glad that we are now tackling the problems after too many years of misguided goodness have been left to do. I am quite sure that future generations will thank us, especially those who had the prospect of growing up in an area where the prospects for, for example, the future of the European Union are very great. the field of education or the labour market would not be very good or where the risk of ending up in crime would be significantly higher than in the surrounding society. These are the children that we provide far better conditions for doing well in the future, not as new dancers, but just as Danes. That is why housing companies now have better opportunities to throw out criminals. Crime in ghetto areas is being severely punished. Residents on transfer income will not be able to move in, all to clean up and create a more mixed housing mix. But don't the problems just move? Some may ask. But it's not like that. For the sum of crime and the sum of social problems are not constant. In fact, the concentration of ethnic groups and socially vulnerable unemployed and criminals in themselves is self-enhancing and creates a negative spiral. Therefore, the solution is to create a more mixed household composition. That in itself will solve a great deal of the problems. That is also why this agreement provides great resources, so that, among other things, we can do something about it. strengthen the efforts around the children and ensure a good start to life. I think that there are a great many good initiatives and, of course, we support the legislative proposal.

ParlaMint-DK_20181011142549	Thank you to the SF rapporteur. Then we have reached the end of the rapporteur's line, and so we can give the floor to the Minister for Transport, Building and Housing. Here you go, Minister.
ParlaMint-DK_20181011142559	Thank you for that and thank you for the good debate. On March 1, 2018, the government presented the • A Denmark without a parallel society • No ghettos in 2030.• On the basis of the initiative, the government has entered into agreements with the Social Democrats, the Danish People's Party and the Socialist People's Party on initiatives in the housing field that counteract parallel societies, and on the prohibition of moving into the hardest ghetto areas for benefit recipients in the cash assistance system. The parties to the agreement agree that there should be no ghettos in Denmark by 2030, and this bill lays the foundations for us to get rid of the ghettos in Denmark once and for all. I would like to take this opportunity to thank the parties behind the agreement, here too, from the platform, for their constructive approach and the good cooperation that we have had and hopefully will also have in this area. We are addressing a major and complex social problem. This is reflected in the draft law, which contains many important initiatives. Over the next many years, the bill will have to bring about a significant change in a wide range of residential areas, which are currently facing massive problems. This will undoubtedly be a long, cool move, but I am convinced that with the bill we will provide municipalities and housing organisations with the instruments needed to put the ghettos to death. Sometimes there are examples that we in here at Christiansborg make some laws and rules, and then maybe less attention is paid to what comes out of it afterwards, i.e. what effect the effort has. I would like to take this opportunity to make it clear that this is not going to happen with this effort. Together with the circle of agreements, I will keep an eye on the work being done and the development going the right way, because we need to get rid of the ghettos. We must be sure that the efforts are directed at those areas where the problems are most serious, and that is why the draft law provides for an adjustment of the ghetto criteria. The four criteria, as they are today, relate to the labour market, crime, education and income. A exposed residential area is proposed to be delimited to residential areas where at least two of the four criteria are met. A ghetto area means a exposed residential area where the proportion of immigrants and descendants from non-western countries exceeds 50%. Finally, a new category, so-called hard ghetto areas, which are residential areas, has been on the ghetto list for the past four years. A transitional arrangement is proposed in this respect. In 2018-2020, a residential area should have been on the ghetto list for the last 5 years in order to be characterised as a hard ghetto area. The transitional arrangements will allow local actors to make additional efforts, allowing the initiatives already under way in a number of residential areas to have an impact which is reflected in the statistical data. The draft law contains initiatives aimed at preventing the emergence of new vulnerable housing areas that could develop into ghetto areas. There is a need to change the composition of residents in the vulnerable residential areas, which are characterised by the fact that a high proportion of residents are outside the labour market, that many are on transfer income, and that a large proportion of residents originate in non-western countries. Today, local authorities must not direct housing seekers to ghetto areas or areas with many unemployed people, if they have been convicted of crime, have been evicted from their homes because of house-order violations or come from a non-western country. The draft law will apply to all vulnerable residential areas, and the municipalities will not be allowed to assign residents on transfer income to a vacant dwelling in these residential areas in future. In addition, it is made compulsory for municipalities to apply the rules on flexible rental in vulnerable residential areas, so that housing seekers who are in work or in training are at the forefront of the queue for a dwelling. In addition, home-seekers for cash benefits, integration benefits or educational assistance must not move into a hard ghetto. In addition, the sale of general housing may help to change the composition of the dwelling in a general residential area. In order to make housing in vulnerable residential areas more marketable, it is proposed to allow housing organisations to terminate tenants in connection with the sale of housing to private persons. In this context, it is proposed that the landlord should offer the tenant a replacement residence. Crime creates great insecurity, especially when it is committed in the area and by people living there. It is not fair to be able to terrorise an entire residential area. It must therefore be easier to expose tenants who have committed serious crime. The same applies in cases where members of a tenant's household are convicted of such crime. A new ground for cancellation is therefore proposed so that a rental relationship can be terminated where tenants or members of tenants' households are punished with an unconditional prison sentence for unsafe crime. The condition is that the crime is committed within 1 km of the property in which the person concerned lives. This is, for example, the case. on gross vandalism, burglary, violence, threats, extortion, robbery, arson, rape and killing. In order to avoid the development of several areas into vulnerable residential areas or ghettos, the proposal provides for the possibility for the highest authority of the housing organisation to take appropriate action against the formation and maintenance of vulnerable residential areas. As a supervisory authority, the municipal council will also have better opportunities to intervene and to impose interventions in vulnerable residential areas. In addition, it is proposed to make it more attractive to sell properties in vulnerable residential areas. New possibilities are provided for the use of proceeds from sales in vulnerable residential areas for improvement and rent reductions in other vulnerable residential areas. The sale of general buildings in one of the hard ghetto areas allows the proceeds to be used for a special basic purchase grant for the construction of general housing. This will enable new general buildings to be built in areas where basic prices are too high to build general housing today. In addition, it is possible for municipalities to waive the requirement for additional purchases in the vulnerable residential areas. There are some residential areas that have been struggling with great challenges for a number of years and that have not yet been able to reverse the negative spiral. In these residential areas there is a need for a significant change, such as: is about reducing the number of general family homes in order to ensure a mixed housing form and a mixed housing mix. These areas must be converted into attractive residential areas. This must be done through, for example. the sale, demolition or new construction of private dwellings and the supply of services. It is therefore proposed that all hard ghetto regions should be included in a development plan. The objective of the development plan is to reduce the proportion of general family housing to a maximum of 40%. By 2030. It is the housing organisation and the municipality that must together make the plan, which must indicate how the target is achieved. At the same time, it is proposed that, exceptionally, when a number of conditions are met, the Minister for Transport, Building and Housing may approve a reduction in the proportion of general family dwellings to a proportion higher than 40%. In agreement with the parties behind the agreement on parallel societies, I intend to table an amendment allowing the municipal council to draw up a development plan if the housing organisation and the municipal council cannot agree. I have sent a proposal to that effect in external consultation. It is also proposed to allow the Housing Minister to decide that hard ghetto areas should be completely settled as a general residential area. For some ghetto areas, the challenges of parallel societies are so massive that it is both practical and economical that it is most appropriate to dismantle the ghetto area and start over. It is therefore proposed that the Minister of Housing may require the dismantling of a hard ghetto area. If the injunction is not complied with, the Minister will be able to take the necessary steps for a state takeover of the area in order to dismantle it. A state takeover will be carried out by law. It will therefore be the Folketing which decides on the State's acquisition of a residential area and the terms of the acquisition. Then I would like to thank you once again for the positive debate. I look forward to the committee reading in the same positive spirit. Thank you for the floor.

ParlaMint-DK_20181011145145	Thank you for that, President. The draft law we are discussing here is about the funds committed to the Landsbygningsfonden in the vulnerable residential areas in the coming years. The government played out with a package to begin with, which meant that 12 billion would be needed. kr. in the exposed residential areas, and in addition to that, which is important to remember was another pool • it had to be taken 13 billion. kr. through the so-called grant aid from the Landsbygningsfonden, which would then be transferred to the Treasury. This would have been an undermining of the general sector from the perspective of the Social Democratic Party as a quality alternative to private housing, and I believe that this would have meant in the long term that a lower standard of housing had been achieved and that the tenants had had to pay a higher rent in the individual departments. That is why we have been opposed from the outset to changing the grant aid, which would cost the 13 billion of the tenants' savings. In addition, we were sceptical about 12 billion. kr. was the right place to set aside for the vulnerable residential areas. We're happy with the solution it ended up with. Ten billion will be set aside. kr. for vulnerable residential areas, which year by year is exactly the same amount as has been allocated since 2011. The money goes to extremely important purposes. Half is spent on renovations in the area, where there is a large backlog in many places, one third is spent on rent reductions and housing social interventions, and approx. One eighth is spent on infrastructure in residential areas. It is money which is spent on the tenants and their properties, their homes in the general sector, as originally intended by the Landsbygningsfonden. There has been a discussion about whether to maintain the funds for housing social work. In principle, I and the Social Democratic Party believe that it is a municipal task to make housing social efforts. It is something that needs to be done locally, it is not necessarily the task of the Landsbyfund to allocate money to it. But, on the other hand, we must also recognise that the practice that has developed over recent years is that it is the Landsbygningsfonden that pays for housing social contributions. Therefore, the removal of funds for housing social interventions will mean that in many places you will not be able to afford to continue with the municipalities where you do not have so much money to spend. That is why we have ended up with a solution in this bill, which lowers the framework slightly, so that there is money for renovations and for rent reductions and infrastructure, but thus still retains the majority of funds for housing social interventions. In addition, the total parallel society agreement has found approx. 2 billion kr. over the contract period to finance day-care institutions, schools, job interventions in the vulnerable residential areas. We in the Social Democratic Party also regard this as an important investment by the state on top of the Landsbyfund's own resources. And with those words, I would like to say that the Social Democrats support the proposal.

ParlaMint-DK_20181011152543	Thank you to the rapporteur. There are no brief comments, so I give the floor to the Minister. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20181011152552	Thank you for that and thank you for the positive reception of the bill. The bill is the implementation of the government's agreement with the Social Democratic Party, the Danish People's Party, the Radical Left and the Socialist People's Party on the financing of interventions to prevent and break down parallel societies and the framework of the Landsbygningsfonden for physical changes to the vulnerable residential areas etc. for the period 2019-2026. The bill must be seen in the context of the bill on new housing initiatives in order to counteract the formation of parallel societies. A total of 10 billion will be allocated. kr. in the Landsbygningsfonden (Landsbygningsfonden) in the period 2019-2026 to make radical physical changes to the affected residential areas as well as to finance a housing social effort. Overall, the bill ensures that substantial funds are allocated in the Landsbygningsfonden to make significant physical changes to the vulnerable residential areas. Thus, the bill supports the objective of not having ghetto areas by 2030. Physical interventions must be accompanied by a housing social effort in the areas at risk, a housing social effort which can prevent extremism and crime and support positive social development in the areas at risk. Let me briefly outline the framework for the individual areas of the legislative proposal. Firstly, we are setting up an investment framework of a total of 5 billion. kr. in the years 2021-2026 to carry out renovation and improvement interventions in vulnerable residential areas. Secondly, priority is given to infrastructure efforts in vulnerable residential areas in order to ensure that isolation can be broken. Thirdly, a framework is provided for housing social interventions and rent subsidies. In doing so, we continue to focus on housing social efforts. Fourthly, the demolition of general housing is an important tool in the physical effort to radically transform vulnerable residential areas. That is why a framework is set aside for demolitions. The proposal provides for the necessary funds in the Landsbygningsfonden for a comprehensive transformation of the vulnerable residential areas in Denmark. Let me make it clear that the legislative proposal focuses on ensuring the financing of precisely those initiatives designed to counteract the formation of parallel societies in vulnerable residential areas. This does not mean that we do not also focus on funding renovation efforts widely in the general housing sector. We have a number of general housing around the country, which is located in areas that are not exposed but need to be renovated. They must of course also be renovated with the support of the Landsbygningsfonden. There is a need for us to have a better basis for assessing the need for the renovation of the general sector, and that is why it is part of the political agreement that an analysis of renovation needs is being carried out in the general sector. The objective of the analysis is to determine the need for supported and unsupported renovations. It is intended that the analysis should form the basis for future priorities of the funds in the Landsbygningsfond for renovation and demolition. It has already been agreed that the parties to the agreement will meet at the end of the analyses and agree on a framework for the renovation and demolition of general housing for the period 2021-2026. I would like to conclude by thanking you once again for the positive comments. I look forward to a committee hearing in the same positive spirit.

ParlaMint-DK_20181011155030	Thank you. Now I was one of them. in the friendly mood of the previous bill. I can say it's gonna be a little different with this bill. The legislative proposals that we are working together with today translate the two agreements that form the basis of the narrow media settlement that the government and the Danish People's Party entered into before the summer holidays. Compared to the media agreement we are discussing today's first part of, the Social Democratic Party had prepared for us to find answers to the increasing pressure on the Danish media that major global players such as Facebook, Amazon and several others put on Danish-language news dissemination and public service activities. After our analysis, it would be crucial for Danish news dissemination and public service that we strengthened it and secured that place in the global structure. We had the approach of tradition to conclude a broad agreement with the Danish Parliament's parties on media agreements as a guarantor of the Danish cultural basis in media and news dissemination. It was with deep regret that we had to note that these interests divided the government and the Danish People's Party not to be spared. It was established and locked in an economic agreement between the government and the Danish People's Party even before negotiations on the media agreement had begun. There was to be a focus on Denmark's Radio, and we realized that the focus is on Denmark's Radio saving 900 million. kr. on the public service, massive savings that mean less Danish content, well notice in order to be able to provide tax relief. In our opinion, this is a skewed priority at a time when the Danes are being flooded with foreign content and platforms, and when the ad crowns on the Danish market are increasingly being served by foreign players, and where fake news is something we must take very seriously in relation to democratic formation. At such a time, we need to stick to what we share, and this is especially true of our public service. This means that we have good and credible Danish media content, which all Danes have free and equal access to, and that we have strong media with public service content, which appeals widely to the Danes, and which helps to strengthen Danish language and culture. The Danish Government and the Danish People's Party have chosen to go in the opposite direction with significant cuts in the size of 900-million crowns, a major deterioration in supply to the Danes and less Danish content. In addition to tax cuts, media conciliation instead increases the space for private commercial media, as well as allowing for advertising and user charges on public service media, for which the Danes are already paying. It is an ideological battle with the Danish media model, which for decades has helped finance good Danish media content, all of which have had free and equal access to. With the media conciliation, as I said, the public service is cut by 900 million overall. kr. It is a significant cut-off that means less Danish content, and the consequences are already so small now: fewer radio and television channels, fewer news, less sport, less width. The Danes are faced with a weakened media supply, and at the same time it should be stressed that there is yet another saving round in Danmarks Radio for 2022 and 2023. For social democracy, it is essential that we stick to what we share. Good Danish public service is needed. We must not weaken it, and that is why we do not support the media reconciliation of the government, and we do not support the two legislative proposals. After all, the two legislative proposals translate parts of the agreement on which this is based, respectively agreement on the focus of Denmark's Radio and the abolition of the media licence, and the agreement on media conciliation. Admittedly, the changes proposed by the conciliation show very well why, overall, the media agreement is moving in the wrong direction. In principle, in relation to the focus on Denmark's Radio and the abolition of the media licence, the Social Democratic Party could support the conversion of the funding of the public service from a licence fee to a tax financing, but the way it is done is quite wrong, because it is used first of all to make a massive cut on the public service part. Secondly, a number of changes are being made which highlight precisely how media conciliation is moving in the wrong direction by weakening our common media. The media settlement means that the Danes can look forward to more user charges and advertisements in connection with content they have already helped finance from the tax. Both the new radio channel and the TV channel will be able to base themselves on advertising revenue, and FM 4 will be re-bid with the possibility of making user charges on podcast, and there may also be advertisements here. It is a great deterioration when the Danes have now paid for the content through the tax. At a time when there is a need for good Danish content and strong media that strengthens our culture, language and community, it is quite wrong to make historical cuts to public service and thus give the Danes a lower media supply. Overall, therefore, the Social Democratic Party cannot support the legislative proposals.

ParlaMint-DK_20181011161830	Thank you. These laws that we are working together today have several things in them. The main objective is to implement those parts of the media agreement which were made before the summer holidays and which require amendment of the law. The great thing is, of course, the abolition of the licence and the way we fund the public service in Denmark, so that over the coming years it will be financed from the tax. We also change the rules for the appointment of Danish Radio's board members. We empower the Minister for Culture to offer new radio and television channels on the basis of the new media agreement and to make a re-supply of FM 4 which is currently operated by Berlingske in the form of Radio24syv. After all, much has already been said on the part of the Social Democrats about the cuts in Denmark's Radio. It is true that the Danish People's Party wants cuts on Danish Radio, and we do so for several reasons. Firstly, we want it to be cheaper to be a Dane in general, and that is why we are abolishing the licence and, overall, we are going to charge less for public service over tax. We believe that when you abolish the licence and instead fund it in the other way, it becomes more socially fair. Secondly, we have also wanted to give more space to the Danish private media. Today the Danish private media have been pressured on one side by the foreign media such as Facebook and Google and on the other side of Denmark's Radio, which has had a very large amount of money to do so. We have found this to be an unequal competition. Thirdly, we also want Denmark's Radio to become more nationalist and to be present throughout the country and not only to be concentrated in the metropolitan area. In other words, we have wanted what we have called a more focused Danish Radio. Well, it is a smaller Danish Radio, but it is still gone a large Danish Radio, which in the coming years, when the savings rounds are over, will still have over 3 billion. kr. a year to do good and which they are sure to get. Then we still have a wish to Denmark's Radio that they should deliver things that private media cannot and dare to do, and that is also why we should all be involved in funding public service. So now we're gonna get rid of blackseers, too. The Danish People's Party still wants a diverse media image, but a media image with better space for both the private media and the local and regional media. Because we actually like Denmark's Radio. We do not like a large Danish Radio or a large and expensive Danish Radio, and that is also why we have made an agreement with the government that in the coming years Denmark Radio will be financed by 20%. less money than they have been given so far. The new public service contract drawn up by DR and the Ministry of Culture has also set the framework for the future activities of Danmarks Radio. The frames are set and Denmark's Radio will fill out the framework. When we in the Danish People's Party back in August 2016 presented our media releases and our proposals for the future media landscape, it was precisely with a desire for a better balance between the state and private media in Denmark and a Danish Radio that focuses more on public service and allows private Danish media and private players to focus more on content on market conditions. After all, we are pleased with the agreement we had made with the government: the abolition of the licence, the saving of 20%. and the media agreement itself back in June. From the first infancy discussions and until now it has been a clear case for the Danish People's Party to change the media image we have known so far. That is why, of course, we are also interested in how Denmark's Radio's activities should be prioritised in the future. Denmark's Radio is focused with fewer channels, less unequal competition between DR and private media and more Danish music on the FM channels. And we would also like to see more content coming from the province. Denmark's Radio will be required to get more around the country. We see this as something positive. The government, in its media report back in April, had planned for the FM signal to be turned off on radio. Fortunately, we got rid of that with many other things that we can get back to. In addition, I have already mentioned that the new public service contract actually states that the FM music channels such as P3 and P4 must increase their percentage of Danish language or Danish-produced music to 48%. We believe this to support the market, and we believe that this is also a public service task. Furthermore, Denmark's Radio will continue to offer high quality digital content as a contribution to DR's public service objectives. DR also still has to go, although some do not believe it, bringing text-based news, but they must leave space digitally and online to the other media. Last but not least, as I have said, DR must strengthen its regional focus and be present throughout the country, not only in DR's regions, which fortunately were freed from savings, but DR must therefore also generally be more present on all platforms throughout the country. In other words, in the eyes of the Danish People's Party, the future slimmer Danish Radio will benefit all Danes in all regions of Denmark, and therefore the Danish People's Party naturally supports both draft laws.

We live in a time when the media market is constantly changing, a change in which the Danes become more digital, both in newspaper reading, TV viewing, streaming of series and films and radio listening. We need to ensure strong Danish media content on all platforms. We need to make sure that Denmark as a whole has modern, varied and strong public service offerings with a focus on core public service, and we need to ensure that there is a balance between state and private media, because private players contribute greatly to good Danish informative content, i.e. a diverse and pluralist media content. And in particular the latter part of the Danish Parliament's parties has tended to overlook. There are many very good new initiatives in the new media agreement. The parts requiring direct legislative changes are simplified provisions of the Radio and Television Act, a new composition of DR's Board of Directors, an extension of the authority of the Minister for Culture in relation to the TV 2 regions and the provision of two new public service channels. The latter is quite important because it contributes to diversity and pluralism. But there are so many other good elements in the agreement that I would like to mention here. We're gonna get a better balance. We do this by strengthening regional and local coverage on the daily page. We will strengthen the ability of newspapers to become more digital and thus meet the future. We are doing this by starting a VAT exemption for digital media, just as there is in the print today. Over the next 5 years, we will have a larger public service pool so that more players can produce Danish public service content. Overall, this is a very good and modern media agreement, and I am happy to announce that the Left supports the two proposals that we are cooperating with here today.

This does not change the fact that one billion people are basically taken out of the media market, and it will also have a negative impact on the regions.
ParlaMint-DK_20181011175123	Then it's mr. Morten Marinus for his second brief remark, but he apostates. Thank you. Then there's no more on the list. We thank the rapporteur, and then we go on to the rapporteur of the Conservative People's Party, Mr. Naser Khader. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20181011175136	Today we are discussing two legislative proposals, L 36 and L 37: L 36 on the transfer of the license to a media tax and L 37 on the media agreement 2019-2020. I will deal with those elements of the media agreement that require amendment of the law. First, the new media agreement will strengthen the Board of Directors of Danmarks Radio. I think it is extremely important that a billion-dollar company like DR has a strong and professional board of directors, understood in that way that someone is appointed with good media and board experience. Therefore, we change the way the board is elected, so that the six largest parties in the Folketing each nominate two candidates for DR's Board of Directors, after which the Minister of Culture and the six parties jointly appoint 6 members to the Board of Directors. We are also introducing a power to offer new broadcasting channels. For example, we agreed in the media agreement that a new cultural television station should be set up. The overall purpose of media conciliation is to create a more differentiated media image. That is why we are providing a new television channel, hoping that it can demonstrate, in the same way as Radio24sy, that you can therefore make quality public service outside Denmark's Radio. We believe, in fact, that supply creates more value for tax dollars. Therefore, we are convinced that it is part of the solution if we continue to offer quality and content to the Danes. Radio24syv has been successful, but the broadcasting licence expires, and therefore the channel must be put back in tender. It will be under some other conditions. Radio24seven has had many means to launch the shipment. That will be adjusted. A new cultural radio channel is also being offered to focus on culture and classical music. So we must now implement much of the media agreement that we have negotiated this year. It is a media agreement that strengthens the diversity of the Danish media landscape. An important element of the agreement is also that we reschedule the licence. Time's running out of the license. There is virtually no licence obligation. Therefore, it does not make sense that the licence is not charged on an equal footing with other taxes. Therefore, it has been possible to make an agreement on the license with the Danish People's Party, so the license is changed. With this change of law we take a fight with the approx. 260,000 licensed households who cheat DR and the rest of us every year by not contributing to the public service common fund. This also avoids the fact that DR has an unnecessary cost of licensing and campaigning. By changing the collection from licence payment to tax, the license will be more fair to all of us. Single people no longer have to pay the same as a household consisting of several people, and at the same time everyone will contribute to our common public service. No one can outrun the bill anymore. Does the tax license mean we're going to turn the license tax up and down? No, of course not. So far, the size of the licence has been adopted by means of multiannual media agreements, and this will also continue in the future. It is important to make it clear that the change from licence financing to finance finance will not change the fundamental independence of Denmark's Radio or the citizens' payment to DR. There is a group of people who do not enjoy Denmark's Radio as much as others, and it is blind and strongly weak. That is why we are also placing a pool of initiatives targeted at this group. In addition, we will offer a special media check to the elderly who have previously paid a reduced licence. So, all in all, it is a good restructuring of a system that does not make much sense anymore, and of course we support it all.
ParlaMint-DK_20181011175630	Thank you. There are no brief comments, so we thank the rapporteur. And then we give the floor to the Minister of Culture.
ParlaMint-DK_20181011175657	Thank you for that and thank you for a lively and passionate debate. This suggests that there are important issues at stake, and I certainly think there are too. Free and diverse media are essential in a democracy. Free media are the salt of democracy and, of course, diversity is absolutely crucial. There have been a great many comments here during this debate, and we have been very, very much around, and we have also been much more far-reaching than what the two legislative proposals are actually proposing. But these are important discussions, and we have already had an open consultation on this and a new one has been called. I come to all the consultations that it has to be, and I am happy to answer questions from now on. But in the first place, I would like to try to stick to what the legislative proposals are really about. So it is not because I do not want to take the other debate; I am happy to do so. But L 36 is about focusing on DR and the abolition of media licenses. The government parties and the Danish People's Party have found that the media license, as it is today, is socially unbalanced. Individuals today have to pay the same as households with more people, although larger households typically have a higher media consumption. So in the current system it is also the case that there are a very large number of viewers who do not contribute to the financing of the joint public service, and that is why on 16 March 2018 the government concluded an agreement with the Danish People's Party to focus DR and abolish the media license; we were simply given an economic framework. And here I would like to make a small comment on Mr. Jacob Mark, who asked if it was not entirely new to say that there was a premise before going into the room where the overall media agreement was to be made. When Mr. Uffe Elbæk was the Minister of Culture at the time, it was announced that it was a premise that TV 2 should not be put up for sale. So there are examples of this, and I think there are many other policy areas too. You might think that's good or bad. It's a perfectly legitimate discussion to have... but it's not the first time, even when we're talking about media negotiations. The draft law here replaces the media licence, and gradually we have a transition in which we make less regulation of the personal allowance for anyone who has reached the age of 18, and that means that with this change, black viewers can no longer avoid contributing to the financing, just as we can say that, in particular, single people will achieve a relaxation. Today there are certain groups of pensioners who can have the media licence reduced by half, and with this bill we are introducing a new media check for pensioners who are currently entitled to a reduced licence. It is also the case that blind and highly partially sighted people today can be exempted from media licenses. In isolation, the change that we are proposing here will not take account of that particular group, and that is why we have set aside a pool for initiatives aimed at blind and highly visually impaired at 10 million. kr. Annually from 2019. The media licence will be fully abolished by 2022 and the gradual transition implies that, during a transitional period until 31 December 2021, DR and the regional TV 2 companies will receive a State grant that will increase in the period 2019-2022, while the institutions will continue to be funded by licence fees that will decrease over the same period. The financial framework for financing DR has been laid down in the political agreement and incorporated into the bill. The conversion from licence financing to finance law funding does not alter the basic position and independence of DR and regional TV2 companies. The political and economic framework will therefore continue to be established through multiannual media agreements for the entire media sector and through public service contracts. It is a concern that has been raised many times and I do not think there is any reason for that concern. It will not be the case that we can start touching these things from one year's Finance Act to the next year's Finance Act. Then there is L 37 on the implementation of parts of the media agreement. On 29 June 2018 the Danish Government and Dansk Folkeparti signed a media agreement for the period 2019-2023, i.e. a 5-year period. As has been mentioned, the main objective of the draft law is to implement four aspects of this media agreement. Firstly, it is proposed to simplify the general provisions of the Radio and Television Act on the public service activities in Denmark. After that, only the permanent public service institutions such as DR, the regional TV 2 companies and TV 2 will be specifically mentioned in the law. Other public service providers do so on the basis of a licence issued by the Radio and Television Board. It is therefore proposed to introduce an authorisation allowing the programme activities of these enterprises to be included in the overall public service activities in accordance with the specific provisions of the Minister for Culture. Secondly, it is proposed to change the rules for the appointment of the DR Executive Board, as several of the rapporteurs have also mentioned. The aim is to strengthen DR's board of directors, also in terms of independence and composition of competence. So with the bill, the six largest parties in the Folketing will each nominate two candidates for DR's Board of Directors, after which the Minister of Culture and the six parties jointly appoint six members to the Board of Directors. Thirdly, it is proposed that the current authority of the Minister for Culture to lay down rules on regional TV 2 companies should be extended to include supervision and sanctions. And that point has nothing to do with the media agreement, but it has to be seen in the wake of a report by the State auditors on the regional TV 2 companies. Fourthly and finally, it is proposed to introduce an authorisation for the provision of new public service channels. The intention, in accordance with the media agreement, is that a new radio channel and a new television channel will be offered again to enhance diversity. The other points of the media agreement will either be implemented administratively, for example. in the form of public service contracts or contract notices, or be implemented by a subsequent amendment of the law, e.g.: the rules on the public service pool and media support. With these comments, I should just like to say that I look forward to the continued discussion and, of course, I am available for the committee's consideration.

One of the concrete initiatives was to target and strengthen the National Science Centre, which is today called Astra, and we do so with this bill. But the whole strategy was large and comprehensive • 180 million. kr. When a child enters his first class in the natural sciences, what is to happen there, and how to build on that professionalism? There I have set up a group with researcher Anja Andersen in the lead who will discuss exactly that, i.e.: How do we create the inner context in the subjects? And when they have finished thinking, there are some professional teachers who have to take over, and in this way I hope to have a very lively trade debate on the natural sciences. There is also an extended talent strategy for pupils who are particularly interested in the natural sciences, which should mean not only that, as today, as a skilled natural science student, you can enter Astra and get one on the experiencer, but also that teaching material that can be brought into the classroom can also be obtained, so that pupils who are particularly skilled and particularly interested can also be met by their teachers while they are in the classroom. In addition, there is an initiative with the so-called supernatural science teachers, where we provide an even longer course of science training for specially interested teachers. So there are many different good things in the package, but what we are discussing here is Astra. And I would like to thank the parties that support the bill. Astra's task is to develop, collect and disseminate scientific knowledge to primary and secondary schools throughout Denmark. This is happening, among other things. through targeted educational programmes, talent competitions and talent programs. The Centre has undergone a number of organisational changes since its creation by law in 2009. In 2015 Astra was merged with the Danish Science Factory and in 2017 with Science Talents at Sorø Academy School. And it is natural that there should be changes to a national science centre, because the dissemination of natural science is an area that develops and new needs arise for what such a centre should be able to do. With this bill, we update and update the legal basis of the National Science Centre. The current description of the Centre's tasks and objectives reflects the needs that were current when the Centre was set up in 2009, and with the Bill we provide the Centre with some more general purpose and task descriptions. This will reflect much more the current form of the centre. At the same time, we ensure the need for flexibility in legislation over time. This will allow the centre to adapt continuously to the way in which they carry out and handle the tasks. In addition, the bill will strengthen cooperation between the Ministry of Education and the Centre. The Centre's management must be responsible for more strategic planning. This includes, among other things: a annual programme with a plan for the activities and areas of action of the coming years. I look forward to developing a strengthened and more modern science centre which can continue to contribute to the dissemination of science knowledge to a lot of students around the country's schools and youth education programmes. We need to put the spotlight on the interest of children and young people in science. Thank you for the floor.

ParlaMint-DK_20181011190945	With this proposal, you can say that changes have been brought together in two different areas, which basically have no other connection than that both are changes to the primary school law. On the other hand, we can see that in their consultation answer the Library Association considers that the proposal can provide opportunities to maintain library access and longer opening hours locally. So we really think that there may be grounds for doubt as to whether this proposal is appropriate. We have seen an unusual number of library dismantlings around the country, and we therefore have a concern that this could pave the way for it, and we think that the signals we receive from the consultation parties are different. Our reservations about the second part of the proposal, which concerns the creation of talent classes in music, are, on the other hand, somewhat greater. After all, the proposed change involves opening up special admission requirements to one of the subjects of the primary school, where admission is made conditional on the pupils having special musical conditions. It is our view in the Enhedslisten that we should have a primary school where all pupils have equal access to the school's subjects and cannot risk being met by sorting mechanisms on the basis of the pupils' special talents. It is, after all, the second subject in which we are now on the way to introducing some kind of restriction on access, and we are frankly very sceptical that this proposal should be put forward. It can be said that if there was free and open access to the talent classes, it was one thing, but it is quite clear in the bill that it should be at all classes from the nursery school to the 9th grade that there can be both special admission requirements and special admission tests. We just don't like that, that is to say, the first day you start in the nursery class, first class, you can be met by special admission tests in relation to this track. One might say: is it a great disaster that you have it? No, but we think it is a slippery slope that, as I said, another subject is restricted and can be restricted by admission requirements and admission tests, and that is why we cannot support that part of the proposal.

So there are actually five needle eyes. The estimate, the assessment, from the Ministry is that it will mean that approx. 180 students are going to go back. We will not know if it will last for a few years when it will all have to be evaluated. The good thing about this bill is that it also applies to refugee children from 1st to 9th grade, i.e. the children who receive Danish as a foreign language or go into reception classes or are integrated and integrated into the class and receive language help. When the maximum of two years is over, they're gonna go through these language tests themselves, so there's that parallelity, and that's a good thing. For it would not have been very clever if Ahmed, who is a third generation immigrant born and raised in Denmark, and comes in 0th grade, had to pass the language tests, and that Ahmed's parents saw that Mohammed, who has just arrived in the third grade, could go straight into a class without having to go through the same language tests with much worse Danish in the bag. So there's that parallelity, and we're really happy with that. All in all, this is helping the students, it's helping the parents, and it's helping the school manager who has the state in his back so he doesn't have to stand alone with it. Then sanctions will also be introduced against schools that deliver poor results or poor well-being, and this is either by instructions or ultimately, after a long process, decommissioning. And we think it is excellent that it is now the Minister who is taking over that opportunity, instead of the municipalities, and the same will apply to youth education institutions, but with the more mild form, namely that they are prevented from taking up. Then the proposal also provides for the possibility of stopping the payment of children's checks and allowances to parents whose children have an illegal 15 per cent absence. or above within a quarter. And that's excellent. It is then the head of the school who must inform the municipal council and then decide to stop it. One can of course be concerned about whether the whole notification will be delayed or delayed, and we would just like to ask the Minister how she will guarantee that this procedure will not be delayed and that the time horizon will not be exceeded, as it is the child's best interests that are at the forefront. Another question that I have not really been able to find answers to in the notes on the law is, of course, due to myself, because I am a little in doubt about what is happening to schools that have departments. Does the law apply to matricles or does it apply to the institution? One could easily imagine that a school with many departments could twist around this 30-percent rule, so you weren't part of it. But I would like to ask if the Minister had the opportunity to answer whether the law applies to institutions or cadastrals, i.e. whether schools with several departments can get out of their way.

ParlaMint-DK_20181011200244	After all, the draft law here is one in a series which together constitutes the implementation of the so-called parallel social agreements. There is therefore, in general, a lack of sensitivity towards those who know the school very well, a lack of sensitivity which also goes through in relation to the intervention against freedom of the free schools, a lack of sensitivity to the serious consequences of imposing financial sanctions on parents. After all, the proposal not only requires parents to pay for extra language training, but also for them to be able to pay through the withdrawal of child and youth benefits if their children have an excessive absence. We find the proposal here deeply, deeply problematic. We find it problematic that people are prepared to close primary schools over the heads of both parents' boards and municipal councils. All in all, in our view, this is a proposal that is, first and foremost, driven by signal policy, but which, unfortunately, is far from being symbolic, but will have serious negative consequences for many pupils and too many schools. That is why Enhedslisten cannot support the proposal.

ParlaMint-DK_20181011200812	Thank you for the floor. Bill L 60 is, after all, an expiry of the government's initiative to combat parallel societies from March this year and of the political agreements that were subsequently concluded. In the field of education, it concerns the agreement of 9 May between the government, the Danish People's Party and the Social Democratic Party. Lack of language skills when schooling begins is an independent problem. As a result, the individual child has difficulty keeping up with the school professionally, and the linguistic difficulties will typically persecute the child for the rest of his life. They achieve lower grades and lower life opportunities as a result of poor school performance. It is, of course, in itself a right to react politically, because it means that, to put it bluntly, the Danish Parliament is correcting certain conditions which threaten the well-being of the individual, but at the same time bad language skills also affect the school as an institution, and this has been a little overlooked in the debate here. The progression in classes is slowing down and you never reach the level reached in a class where there are no or few who have language difficulties. If there are more than 15%. children of non-western immigrants in a school class, everyone, and I repeat everyone, not just the 15%, receives a lower life pay. A recent study from the think tank Kraka and Deloitte showed this. So if we tolerate the state of things right now, the majority behind the agreement would also tolerate thousands of children who actually speak Danish fluently being placed in a situation that made them poorer than children from well-functioning classes. We cannot, of course, be a part of this, so that is why the L 60 bill. The other elements of the draft law are a consequence of the main objective I have examined. In the first year of the child, it is of course the parents who have full responsibility for the child being put on the right course. It's only a joke when you say you have to choose your parents carefully. That's not how it really works. In Denmark, the right course for a child is that this child speaks Danish fluently, no matter what family background the child grows up in. There is very solid research evidence for this. Therefore, parents must have a financial incentive not to lose their parental responsibility. The last elements of the bill are then about what to do with schools that do not work. The municipalities will have better opportunities to follow up on schools that are developing badly in time, and if a school is completely dysfunctional, it is clear that it should close down, and that is something that we can now have the opportunity to do. It has been said several times that there have been other plans that should lead the parallel lines onto the same track, but this is the best and most thought-out plan to date, and that is why of course the draft law has LA's warm support. Thank you for the floor.

ParlaMint-DK_20181011201423	Thank you. The alternative and radical left are actually quite opposed to the draft law, but we are in favour of finding solutions to the problem. We are opposed to this bill, because it may well have the right intentions, namely to ensure the linguistic development of particularly vulnerable children and to ensure good quality in primary and secondary education, but the draft law is using the wrong means, and this will very likely lead to a number of unfortunate consequences. It is unethical because it exposes young children to testing and the risk of being punished by having to change the kindergarten class if they do not pass. That is, of course, the whole way to organise it. This is discriminatory because only those schools which have a high proportion of children from socially vulnerable residential areas are affected. And it is centralistic because it breaks with Denmark's traditions that it is the municipalities that are responsible for the primary school, and not the state. That is why, in our opinion, it is actually wrong for the Minister, with this bill, to try to put in place the possibility of being able to close down a primary school. ParlaMint-DK_20181011201643	Thank you to the rapporteur. And the next in line is mr. Jacob Mark as rapporteur for the SF.
ParlaMint-DK_20181011201654	In Denmark we are pushing children more and more; that is my very clear view. We're in a hurry to speed them up. Historically, they come faster from crèche to kindergarten now than they have before; they come faster from kindergarten to school now than they have before, because there is money to be picked up by pressing them quickly. When they go to primary school, they have to be tested 10 times, and for those tests you can test twice, and it can then be 30 times in primary school. I don't think it's a coincidence when more children experience anxiety, more children suffer depression. I don't think it's a coincidence when more kids are insecure. Nor do I think it is a coincidence when more children experience what is called school migration, namely that they really want to go to school, but they cannot do so because they cannot get themselves out of bed and into the school they go to. There is a wise person, even wiser than me, who has given these children a name, namely glass children, because they are transparent and joyless. Not all children are like this, I agree, but I see a tendency, and I think there is a tendency for these glass children to become more and more of them, who are insecure and tired of being tested and who do not find the same joy in going to school as children have had in the past. And if you look at the national test in the second grade, which is really perhaps the closest we get to these language tests, then it's proven that there are many children who are seriously miserable with these tests.
