And I do not understand that the government that has changing ministers saying that we are stressful and pushing children too much, so in another context only introduces stop or language tests for six-year-old children. We want this to be abolished, and we want it to be abolished after a choice, and we want to work for it. And we believe that it can be done by a narrowly possible majority. It has been introduced by a majority bypassing conciliation, which is why we can also abolish it again as soon as there is a red majority bypassing conciliation.
ParlaMint-DK_20181011202135	Thank you to the rapporteur. Then the trip has come to the Minister of Education.
ParlaMint-DK_20181011202142	Thank you. I think I will start by saying that if there is something that is stressful and pressing for a child, then it is absolutely impossible to keep up with the teaching, then going in a class where those who are in class have some preschool skills, which may be 2 years better than those you have 2 years of age is a lot for children starting at school. These are some of the children we are looking at here who are so far behind in their preschool skills. These are some of the same children who go all the way through the Danish primary school and go out with a character cut that is far below the average far below. They start with such a bad language, a vocabulary, an understanding of the language that they are not able to participate in teaching on equal terms with their classmates, and this is a situation that has been going on for not 1 year or 2 years, but rather for 10, 20, 30 years. So those who are worried about whether there are some children here who are being stressed or pressed, I would just like to remind you of the situation that children have been put in in these areas year after year for so many years. And there have been good adults, and there has been freedom, and there has been commitment, and there have been good wills, but it has just not been resolved. It's not well, but back to the playout here. In March of this year, we presented the playout "One Denmark without a Parallel Society" • no ghettos in 2030 • and the playout consists of a number of initiatives targeted at the places in Denmark where parallel societies are most common. The whole exercise actually resulted from a discussion we had on education, in which I suggested that we proceed in a more holistic way. And so we did. We made a parallel society package which deals with a number of different areas, and we presented it in Mjølnerparken, which is a place where I myself have come very much, namely because my own son has gone to the crèche there. After we presented the show, I went to the crèche to greet some of the educators who still remember me from then on, and it was a happy reunion, and we had a good talk. But the leader over there was a little bit concerned about it, because she thought it was a little weird that such a minister came into the crèche, and I quite understand that. I understand that many of the people who live in these areas are thinking: what kind of people come in here and want us something? But the challenge is that if we do not want them something, if we do not put in here, things will just go on as they are, and we simply cannot be familiar with that. We must take initiatives in those areas where previous efforts have not been made. We must prevent and break down parallel societies, and we must do so in a way that does not undermine the rights and freedoms we have in Denmark at the same time. It is a difficult balancing process, and it takes a very precise effort to ensure that we do not bother all Danes throughout Denmark, but put in the places where the problems are. And it is precisely this precise and determined effort that we are making possible with the initiatives in the legislative proposal we are discussing today. I would like to thank the parties that support this bill, which translates a number of initiatives from the agreement to combat parallel societies. Being able to speak a good Danish is an extremely important key to integration; it is an obvious prerequisite for managing in school, both professionally and socially. Early language learning must take place in the home, and it is the parents' responsibility that the child's Danish is sufficient before school starts. However, the proportion of weak second-class readers is greater in primary schools with more than 30%. pupils from vulnerable residential areas, if compared with other primary schools, and this also applies, even if the parents' educational background is taken into account. This will cause major problems in the future, both for the whole of society, but most importantly for each of these children, who are not doing well in school and who are already behind on points from the nursery class. We have to do something about that. That is why this bill proposes to introduce language tests in nursery schools with a high proportion of children from vulnerable residential areas. If a student does not finally pass the test, the child must change the kindergarten class, but it must be the case that the first-class start can only be postponed once. In addition to the language tests, there is also intensive language stimulation for the children who perform the initial test at the start of the nursery class poorly. Such a course can help the child to get a better language, but I would like to stress that the main responsibility will always lie with the parents. It is and remains the parents' job to ensure that their children are ready to start school, and it also involves a good and age-matching language. The school's job is to teach the children the subjects, but it is a prerequisite that the spoken language is taught at home. It is precisely the responsibility of the parents that is at the heart. It is and remains mother and father who are responsible for their children, for having a good and solid breakfast, for coming up in the morning, and not least for having their school. Unfortunately, there are cases where parents fail to fulfil that responsibility, and with the bill we want to strengthen parental responsibility and make it possible to include the children's check in families where children do not participate in primary school education. With the bill we will also introduce a mandatory language test for pupils in the 1st to 9th grade who receive Danish as a second language. This should give the student a guarantee that they get a good benefit from the teaching. Furthermore, we will strengthen sanctions both against primary and secondary schools if they repeatedly deliver poor results. In the end, it must be possible to close down a school or institution if they are not able to raise the quality. When you choose to move to Denmark, you as parents have a responsibility for your children learning Danish before they start school. And you can see that in families where you speak Danish, the children do better. It is important that all children, regardless of their background, make the best of their schooling, and we are well on the way to that with the efforts made in this bill. Thank you for the floor.
